REFINERY NEWS: Brazil's Petrobras shuts Repar refinery for maintenance
Targets FCC, hydrotreaters
Expects to restart mid-November
Investment of $150 million
Brazilian state-led oil company Petrobras will start a maintenance shutdown at the Repar refinery in Parana state on Oct. 5, part of $150 million in investments to modernize and expand the 207,000 b/d facility that supplies refined products for the country's southern region, according to the company.
"These investments reinforce Petrobras' commitment to safety, asset integrity and the operational reliability of Repar," Felipe Leonardo Gomes, the refinery's general manager, said Oct. 2 in a statement. "In addition to preparing the refinery for a new operational cycle, the intervention will allow energy efficiency gains and the installation of improvements that will contribute to the competitiveness and operational results of the refinery."
The shutdown is part of Petrobras' wider expansion efforts that aim to expand overall refinery output to meet about 85% of Brazil's diesel consumption by 2030, according to the company. Under Petrobras' $109 billion strategic investment plan for 2026-2030, the company earmarked $21 billion for investments in its refineries to raise processing capacity by about 320,000 b/d. The investment outlay included $2.4 billion for maintenance projects.
Petrobras will likely further increase refinery investments as part of its 2027-2031 strategic plan, with Petrobras CEO Magda Chambriard saying Sept. 25 that the company will work to meet 100% of Brazil's diesel needs by 2031 in order to improve the country's energy security and tamp down inflation. Diesel is Brazil's top refined production in terms of consumption, with Latin America's largest economy typically importing about 25% of domestic demand each year.
The maintenance work at Repar will target the refinery's Fluid Catalytic Cracker, or FCC, as well as diesel and gasoline hydrotreatment units, Petrobras said. Additional work will be carried out on the refinery's auxiliary units, with more than 1,000 pieces of equipment subject to inspection, maintenance and interventions.
The work is expected to be completed in the second half of November, Petrobras said.

Guarantees LPG, gasoline supply Repar has installed capacity to process about 33,000 cubic
meters of crude oil, or the equivalent of 207,564 b/d of refined products, according to Petrobras. The refinery primarily produced diesel, gasoline, LPG, jet fuel, asphalt, petroleum coke, fuel oil and bunker fuel.
The refinery supplies about 85% of demand in the Parana, Santa Catarina and Mato Grosso do Sul states, as well as the southern portion of Sao Paulo state, according to Petrobras. Excess refined product output is shipped to other states in Brazil or exported.
During the shutdown, the markets supplied by Repar will receive shipments from other refineries via Petrobras' internal logistics system, Gomes said.
"The units focused on LPG and gasoline production will undergo maintenance during the period," Gomes said. "Petrobras carried out planning to ensure demand is met during the shutdown."
-- Jeff Fick
REFINERY NEWS: PDVSA imports lubricants as it repairs Cardon unit
PDVSA accelerates repairs at lubricant plant at its Cardon unit
Imported lubricant base oils come from South Korea
Tanker Linus waits to discharge 10,050 mt of lubricant base oils
Venezuelan state-owned PDVSA is accelerating repairs at the lubricant packaging plant of its Cardón refinery as part of the maintenance and infrastructure recovery program for its national refining system, according to sources.
"According to the schedule, we expect to restart the unit in the second week of October," said a refinery manager who spoke on condition of anonymity.
The maintenance schedule has included repairs to some distillation units, reformers, and catalytic cracking units, according to previous reports.
"Repairs are carried out as financial resources become available to contract the maintenance work."
The lubricant packaging plant has been shut down since 2020. However, the lubricants complex at the Cardon refinery, where base stocks were manufactured, has been shut down since 2012 due to multiple failures, lack of maintenance, and the dismantling of some units, according to a refinery manager. Lubricant bases are used to manufacture commercial lubricating oils for the automotive or industrial sectors for the local market. "Due to the low supply of lubricant-grade crude oil, as well as the failure to comply with maintenance schedules, the operational continuity of the Cardón lubricants complex has been affected, necessitating the importation of base stocks to meet domestic market demand," according to the refinery manager.

© 2026 by S&P Global Inc. All rights reserved.

©2026 S&P Global Inc. Any unauthorized use of this copyrighted material constitutes infringement of our intellectual property and will subject you to possible legal action.

Platts Oilgram Price Report

October 5, 2026

To restart lubricant packaging operations, PDVSA imported lubricant base oils.
The tanker Linus, carrying a cargo of lubricant bases sold to PDVSA by the trader Trafigura, is waiting at the Cardon maritime terminal to unload the following lubricant bases: 950 metric tons of 150N; 5,600 metric tons of 60N; 1,500 metric tons of BS 150, and 2,000 metric tons of 220N, according to a PDVSA marketing official who spoke with Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, on condition of anonymity.
According to S&P Global Commodities at Sea data, the Linus P vessel arrived at the Punta Cardon terminal on Sept. 28 with a shipment of lubricants, having departed the Ulsan terminal in South Korea on Aug. 28.
No data was available on lubricant demand in the Venezuelan market.
The Cardon refinery is located on the Paraguana Peninsula in Falcón State, in western Venezuela.
PDVSA was not immediately available for comment. Sources: Refinery Cardon manager/PDVSA commercial officer .
-- Mery Mogollon
REFINERY NEWS: Vietnam's Nghi Son to operate CDU at 125% capacity until year-end
Refinery: Nghi Son, Vietnam
Overall capacity: 200,000 b/d
Owners: Japan's Idemitsu Kosan (35.1%), Kuwait Petroleum International (35.1%), PetroVietnam (25.1%), Mitsui Chemicals (4.7%)
Vietnam's Nghi Son Refinery and Petrochemical (NSRP) aims to run the crude distillate unit (CDU) at its 200,000 b/d refinery at an average capacity of 125% until the end of this year, NSRP said in a statement Oct. 2.
In the first eight months, the CDU capacity was 110.9%, processing 7.3 million mt (220,202 b/d) of crude oil, NSRP said.
During the period, NSRP earned an estimated after-tax profit of 14 trillion dong ($538.4 million), its first annual profit since commercial start-up in late 2018, NSRP said.
State-owned PetroVietnam has a 25.1% stake in Nghi Son, with Kuwait Petroleum International (35.1%), Japan's Idemitsu Kosan (35.1%), and Japan's Mitsui Chemicals (4.7%).
Nghi Son and the 130,000 b/d Dung Quat are the two oil refineries in Vietnam.
-- Newsdesk-Vietnam
REFINERY NEWS: Bilbao prepares to start up new splitter
Refinery: Petroleos del Norte SA, Bilbao, Spain
Owner: 86% Repsol SA, 14% Kutxabank SA
Overall capacity: 11 million mt/year or 220,000 b/d
The Bilbao refinery operator Petroleos del Norte, SA, or Petronor, said in a note Oct. 2 that it was carrying out

preparations for the start up of a new splitter at the site's fluid catalytic cracker.
The new unit will produce polymer-grade propylene by separating it from propane, it said.
It will be used to feed the company's polypropylene plant in Sines, Portugal, which was planning an upgrade in Q3 2026.
The new unit allows increased propylene purification and was budgeted at 58 million ($67 million).
It will save about 35,000 metric tons/year of CO2 emissions, the company estimates.
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, assessed the three-to 30day forward free-delivered Northwest European polymer grade propylene spot price at 1,043/mt on Oct. 2, up 30/mt day over day and 72/mt week over week.
-- Nayana Khosla, Gianluca Baratti
REFINERY NEWS: Malaysia's Melaka back from maintenance: sources
Refinery: Melaka, Malaysia
Owner: Petronas
Overall capacity: 300,000 b/d
Malaysia's Melaka refinery is back from its planned maintenance works, according to several market sources with knowledge of the matter.
The maintenance work began around mid-August and affects one of the crude distillation units and some secondary units, sources have said previously.
Petronas was not available to provide an official comment. Sources previously said the larger CDU would undergo maintenance work in 2026. Maintenance work was carried out at the smaller 100,000 barrels/day CDU in early 2024.
-- Elza Turner, Jonathan Nonis

Platts proposes amending freight calculation for Oman, Murban CFR North Asia crude assessments Nov. 2
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, is proposing to amend the freight calculation used in its daily Oman and Murban CFR North Asia crude assessments, effective Nov. 2, 2026.
Under the proposed change, Platts would use the daily VLCC Gulf of Oman-to-Far East freight assessment in $/mt (DOOEW00), which was launched Aug. 3, in its calculation of the outright price of Oman and Murban delivered into North Asia at 4:30 pm Singapore time (0830 GMT).
The proposed change would better reflect the shipping economics for the two grades, with Murban primarily loading from Fujairah port and Oman from Mina Al Fahal port outside the Strait of Hormuz.
Currently, the freight calculation is based on the daily VLCC Persian Gulf-to-Far East freight assessment in $/mt (TDAAB00).
The freight calculation would continue to use the conversion factor for metric tons to barrels, as applied to the grades, in line with their prevailing qualities. The conversion factors are currently 7.32 for Oman and 7.62 for Murban.
In line with this change, Platts is also proposing to use the daily VLCC Gulf of Oman-to-Far East worldscale freight assessment (DIOEW00) in the freight calculation for Murban in its Indonesian Minas, Cinta and Widuri crude market parity prices.
Under the current MPP methodology for Minas, Cinta and Widuri, Murban -- as one of five basket grades -- is normalized for delivery to Singapore, accounting for freight. This is currently calculated using the daily VLCC Persian Gulf-to-Singapore

worldscale freight assessment (PFAOD00). The affected assessments appear in Crude Oil Marketwire,
Oilgram Price Report, LNG Daily, Platts OPR World Crude Table, Platts Global Alert/Platts Crude Oil Alert pages 1250, 1805, 2202, 2203, 2211, 2216, 2217, 2244, 2245, 2264, 2265, 2280, 2281, 2811 and 2812, LNG Alert pages 0050 and 0051, Platts Natural Gas Alert pages 1024 and 1069, and under the price database codes below:

Assessments Oman CFR North Asia Murban CFR North Asia Minas Singapore close Minas London close Cinta Singapore close Cinta London close Widuri Singapore close Widuri London close Minas FOB Indonesia $/MMBtu

Daily PCAQJ00 PCAQE00 PCABO00 AAPAZ00 PCAAX00 AAPBJ00 PCAFE00 AAPBN00 LCABO00

Platts to launch daily FOB Middle East bitumen assessments from Nov. 2
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, will launch daily VG30 and VG40 bitumen spot assessments for FOB Middle East, effective Nov. 2, 2026.
The new assessments will complement Platts' existing daily and weekly bitumen assessments, providing greater price transparency and information in the waterborne trade for the product in the Asia-Pacific and Middle East markets.
Platts will launch two new daily outright assessments and their respective monthly averages. These assessments will reflect bulk viscosity grade 30 and VG40 grade bitumen, as per IS 73:2013 specifications, loading 10-30 days forward from the date of publication in the Middle East.

Assessment FOB Middle East Bitumen VG30 FOB Middle East Bitumen VG40

Quantity 2,500-10,000 mt, bulk 2,500-10,000 mt, bulk

Laycan 10-30 days forward 10-30 days forward

UoM $/mt $/mt

Pricing information for material with other specifications, such as PEN 60/70, may be considered for the assessments, normalized to the proposed standard.
The assessments will reflect market value at the close of Asian trading at 4:30 pm Singapore time and follow the Singapore publication schedule. Bids, offers and trades reported throughout the day may be considered in the assessment process, with values normalized to the market close at 4:30 pm Singapore time.
This follows a proposal published Sept. 14 and available here. Please send any questions, comments or feedback to asia_products@spglobal.com, with a copy to pricegroup@spglobal.com.

Platts proposes to amend rollover date of Asian Dated Brent Strip at London close
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, is proposing to amend the rollover date of its Asian Dated Brent Strip at London close assessment to the first publication day of the month, from Dec. 1, 2026.
The proposed change, which follows Platts review of historical data sets, would align the ADB Strip at London close assessment with the current rollover date of its ADB Strip at Asia close assessment.
Currently, this assessment, which reflects the forward value of Dated Brent two months ahead of the date of publication at the 4:30 pm London close, rolls over on the 9th of the month.
The assessments affected by this change appear in Crude Oil Marketwire, Asia Pacific/Arab Gulf Marketscan, Oilgram Price Report, Platts Global Alert/ Platts Crude Oil Alert pages 2206, 2213, 2215, 2217, 2219, 2260, 2261, 2262, 2263, 2264, 2265, 2266, 2267, 2268, 2269, 2270, 2271, 2280, 2281, 2558, 2559, and under their price database codes as below:
